- [ ] **Step 9: Write the failing server-level tests**

Create `tests/test_operation_records.py`:

```python
"""B12 through the server: every call leaves a record; client ids replay or refuse.

``AuditMiddleware`` mints the id into ``CURRENT_OPERATION`` (re-entry through
the search proxy reuses it), ``cad_batch`` mints one per step, and a client's
own id - ``_meta.operation_id`` on any request, ``cad_batch(operation_id=...)``
- replays the recorded result (``replayed: true``), is refused with
``operation_conflict`` for another request and with ``requires_reconcile``
after an ``unknown_commit``. Everything is read back through
``system_status``'s ``operations``.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import threading
import time

import pytest
import pytest_asyncio
from fastmcp import Client
from fastmcp.exceptions import McpError, ToolError

import server
from backends import ezdxf_backend
from backends.document_guard import ActiveDocumentChangedError
from backends.operations import CURRENT_OPERATION

_LINE = {"x1": 0, "y1": 0, "x2": 10, "y2": 0}


@pytest_asyncio.fixture
async def client():
    async with Client(server.mcp) as connected:
        await connected.call_tool("drawing_new", {})
        yield connected


async def _status(client) -> dict:
    return (await client.call_tool("system_status", {})).structured_content


async def _operations(client) -> list[dict]:
    return (await _status(client))["operations"]


async def _entities(client) -> int:
    info = (await client.call_tool("drawing_info", {})).structured_content
    return int(info["entity_count"])


async def _until(predicate, timeout: float = 10.0) -> None:
    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
    while not await predicate():
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            raise AssertionError(f"condition not met within {timeout:.1f}s")
        await asyncio.sleep(0.02)


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_every_call_leaves_a_committed_record_with_its_revisions(client):
    before = await _status(client)
    await client.call_tool("entity_create_line", _LINE)
    rows = [r for r in await _operations(client) if r["tool"] == "entity_create_line"]
    assert len(rows) == 1
    (row,) = rows
    assert row["state"] == "committed" and row["client_supplied"] is False
    assert len(row["operation_id"]) == 32
    assert row["document_id"] == before["document_id"]
    assert (row["revision_before"], row["revision_after"]) == (
        before["revision"],
        before["revision"] + 1,
    )
    assert row["error_kind"] is None and row["ended"] >= row["started"]


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_client_id_replays_instead_of_drawing_twice(client):
    first = await client.call_tool("entity_create_line", _LINE, meta={"operation_id": "line-op-1"})
    second = await client.call_tool("entity_create_line", _LINE, meta={"operation_id": "line-op-1"})
    assert second.structured_content["replayed"] is True
    assert second.structured_content["operation_id"] == "line-op-1"
    assert second.structured_content["handle"] == first.structured_content["handle"]
    assert await _entities(client) == 1
    (row,) = [r for r in await _operations(client) if r["operation_id"] == "line-op-1"]
    assert row["client_supplied"] is True and row["state"] == "committed"


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_the_same_client_id_for_another_request_is_refused(client):
    await client.call_tool("entity_create_line", _LINE, meta={"operation_id": "line-op-2"})
    other = await client.call_tool(
        "entity_create_line",
        {**_LINE, "x2": 20},
        meta={"operation_id": "line-op-2"},
        raise_on_error=False,
    )
    assert other.is_error is True
    payload = other.structured_content
    assert payload["kind"] == "operation_conflict" and payload["operation_id"] == "line-op-2"
    assert payload["reason"] == "different_request" and payload["tool"] == "entity_create_line"
    assert await _entities(client) == 1


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_malformed_client_id_is_refused_before_anything_runs(client):
    result = await client.call_tool(
        "entity_create_line", _LINE, meta={"operation_id": "op\n1"}, raise_on_error=False
    )
    assert result.is_error is True
    assert result.structured_content["kind"] == "invalid_args"
    assert "printable" in result.structured_content["error"]
    assert await _entities(client) == 0


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_batch_with_a_client_id_replays_and_records_its_steps(client):
    steps = [
        {"tool": "entity_create_line", "args": _LINE},
        {"tool": "entity_create_line", "args": {**_LINE, "y1": 5, "y2": 5}},
    ]
    payload = {"steps": steps, "operation_id": "batch-op-1"}
    first = (await client.call_tool("cad_batch", payload)).structured_content
    assert first["ok"] is True and first["operation_id"] == "batch-op-1"
    second = (await client.call_tool("cad_batch", payload)).structured_content
    assert second["replayed"] is True and second["operation_id"] == "batch-op-1"
    assert await _entities(client) == 2, "the replay drew nothing"
    operations = await _operations(client)
    (batch,) = [r for r in operations if r["operation_id"] == "batch-op-1"]
    assert (batch["tool"], batch["state"], batch["client_supplied"]) == (
        "cad_batch",
        "committed",
        True,
    )
    step_rows = [r for r in operations if r["tool"] == "entity_create_line"]
    assert len(step_rows) == 2
    assert all(r["state"] == "committed" and r["operation_id"] != "batch-op-1" for r in step_rows)
    assert step_rows[1]["revision_before"] == step_rows[0]["revision_after"]


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_failed_step_names_its_operation_and_the_batch_is_partial(client):
    result = (
        await client.call_tool(
            "cad_batch",
            {
                "steps": [
                    {"tool": "entity_get", "args": {"handle": "NOSUCH"}},
                    {"tool": "entity_create_line", "args": _LINE},
                ],
                "on_error": "continue",
            },
        )
    ).structured_content
    error = result["results"][0]["error"]
    assert error["kind"] == "failed"
    operations = await _operations(client)
    (step,) = [r for r in operations if r["operation_id"] == error["operation_id"]]
    assert (step["tool"], step["state"], step["error_kind"]) == ("entity_get", "failed", "failed")
    (batch,) = [r for r in operations if r["tool"] == "cad_batch"]
    assert batch["operation_id"] == result["operation_id"]
    assert (batch["state"], batch["error_kind"]) == ("partial", "failed")


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_cancelled_write_is_unknown_commit_and_its_retry_must_reconcile(
    client, monkeypatch
):
    entered, release = threading.Event(), threading.Event()

    async def stuck_circle(self, *args, **kwargs):
        def _sync():
            entered.set()
            assert release.wait(30), "the test never released the worker"
            return "late"

        return await self._async(_sync)

    monkeypatch.setattr(ezdxf_backend.EzdxfBackend, "entity_create_circle", stuck_circle)
    args = {"cx": 0, "cy": 0, "radius": 5}
    request_id = client.session._request_id
    call = asyncio.ensure_future(
        client.call_tool("entity_create_circle", args, meta={"operation_id": "circle-op-1"})
    )
    try:
        assert await asyncio.to_thread(entered.wait, 10)
        await client.cancel(request_id)
        with pytest.raises(McpError, match="cancelled"):
            await call

        async def recorded() -> bool:
            return any(
                r["operation_id"] == "circle-op-1" and r["state"] == "unknown_commit"
                for r in await _operations(client)
            )

        await _until(recorded)
        (row,) = [r for r in await _operations(client) if r["operation_id"] == "circle-op-1"]
        assert row["error_kind"] == "cancelled"
        retry = await client.call_tool(
            "entity_create_circle",
            args,
            meta={"operation_id": "circle-op-1"},
            raise_on_error=False,
        )
        assert retry.is_error is True
        assert retry.structured_content["kind"] == "requires_reconcile"
        assert retry.structured_content["operation_id"] == "circle-op-1"
    finally:
        release.set()


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_a_refusal_names_the_operation_it_ended(client, tmp_path):
    result = await client.call_tool(
        "drawing_save_as", {"path": str(tmp_path / "part.dwg")}, raise_on_error=False
    )
    payload = result.structured_content
    assert payload["kind"] == "unsupported"
    (row,) = [r for r in await _operations(client) if r["operation_id"] == payload["operation_id"]]
    assert (row["tool"], row["state"], row["error_kind"]) == (
        "drawing_save_as",
        "refused",
        "unsupported",
    )


@pytest.mark.asyncio
async def test_the_search_proxy_reuses_the_operation_it_runs_under(monkeypatch):
    seen: list[str | None] = []
    original = ezdxf_backend.EzdxfBackend.entity_create_line

    async def spy(self, *args, **kwargs):
        seen.append(CURRENT_OPERATION.get())
        return await original(self, *args, **kwargs)

    monkeypatch.setattr(ezdxf_backend.EzdxfBackend, "entity_create_line", spy)
    server._apply_discovery_mode("search")
    try:
        async with Client(server.mcp) as client:
            await client.call_tool("call_tool", {"name": "drawing_new", "arguments": {}})
            await client.call_tool("call_tool", {"name": "entity_create_line", "arguments": _LINE})
            status = (
                await client.call_tool("call_tool", {"name": "system_status", "arguments": {}})
            ).structured_content
    finally:
        server._apply_discovery_mode("off")
    (operation_id,) = seen
    rows = [r for r in status["operations"] if r["operation_id"] == operation_id]
    assert len(rows) == 1 and (rows[0]["tool"], rows[0]["state"]) == ("call_tool", "committed")
    assert not any(r["tool"] == "entity_create_line" for r in status["operations"]), (
        "one request, one record"
    )


def test_the_batch_taxonomy_declares_every_kind_1_7_emits():
    assert server.BATCH_ERROR_KINDS == (
        "denied",
        "malformed_step",
        "unknown_tool",
        "invalid_args",
        "unresolved_ref",
        "unsupported",
        "refused",
        "failed",
        "quarantined",
        "busy",
        "stale_revision",
        "active_document_changed",
        "scan_incomplete",
        "unknown_commit",
        "timed_out",
        "cancelled",
        "operation_conflict",
    )


def test_a_document_switch_is_its_own_kind_in_a_batch_row():
    cause = ActiveDocumentChangedError(
        {"document_id": "a" * 32, "name": "First.dwg", "path": None},
        {"document_id": None, "name": "Second.dwg", "path": None},
    )
    wrapped = ToolError("Error calling tool 'entity_create_line': switched")
    wrapped.__cause__ = cause
    row = server._classify_batch_error(wrapped)
    assert row["kind"] == "active_document_changed"
    assert row["bound"]["name"] == "First.dwg" and row["active"]["name"] == "Second.dwg"
```

3. Replace the whole `class AuditMiddleware(Middleware):` (through its final `raise`) with:

```python
class AuditMiddleware(Middleware):
    """Log every tool call with timing, and keep its operation record (B12).

    The record (``backends/operations.py``) is opened before the call and
    closed with an honest state after it: the result, a refusal, an exception,
    or a cancellation - ``unknown_commit`` when a mutating call's worker was
    already running (the backend's ``call_outcomes`` rows carry the id). A
    client's own id (``_meta.operation_id``, or ``cad_batch``'s
    ``operation_id``) replays a recorded result, is refused with
    ``requires_reconcile`` after an ``unknown_commit`` and with
    ``operation_conflict`` for another request. A call re-entering the
    middleware (the search proxy's ``call_tool``) reuses the id already set:
    one request, one record.
    """

    async def on_call_tool(self, context: MiddlewareContext, call_next):
        tool_name = context.message.name
        start = time.monotonic()
        if CURRENT_OPERATION.get() is not None:
            return await self._logged(context, call_next, tool_name, start)
        lifespan = _lifespan_context_or_none(context)
        journal = _operations_journal(lifespan)
        backend = lifespan.get("backend") if lifespan is not None else None
        # getattr: a hand-built context (tests/test_cancel_rollbacks.py) has no arguments.
        arguments = dict(getattr(context.message, "arguments", None) or {})
        effective = _effective_tool(tool_name, arguments)
        try:
            supplied = _client_operation_id(tool_name, arguments, _request_meta(context))
            record, replay = journal.begin(
                tool_name, arguments, operation_id=supplied, document=_identity_of(backend)
            )
        except (OperationConflictError, RequiresReconcileError) as exc:
            elapsed = (time.monotonic() - start) * 1000
            log.warning("TOOL ERR %-40s %6.1fms  (%s)", tool_name, elapsed, exc.kind)
            payload = {**exc.to_dict(), "tool": tool_name}
            return ToolResult(content=str(exc), structured_content=payload, is_error=True)
        except ValueError as exc:
            elapsed = (time.monotonic() - start) * 1000
            log.warning("TOOL ERR %-40s %6.1fms  (invalid operation id)", tool_name, elapsed)
            payload = {"ok": False, "kind": "invalid_args", "error": str(exc), "tool": tool_name}
            return ToolResult(content=str(exc), structured_content=payload, is_error=True)
        if replay is not None:
            log.info("TOOL REPLAY %-37s %s", tool_name, record.operation_id)
            return _replayed_result(replay, record.operation_id)
        token = CURRENT_OPERATION.set(record.operation_id)
        try:
            result = await self._logged(context, call_next, tool_name, start)
        except asyncio.CancelledError:
            journal.finish(
                record,
                _interrupted_state(backend, record.operation_id, effective),
                error_kind="cancelled",
                document=_identity_of(backend),
            )
            raise
        except Exception as exc:
            state, kind = _exception_outcome(backend, record.operation_id, effective, exc)
            journal.finish(
                record, state, result=exc, error_kind=kind, document=_identity_of(backend)
            )
            raise
        finally:
            CURRENT_OPERATION.reset(token)
        state, kind = _result_outcome(effective, result)
        journal.finish(
            record, state, result=result, error_kind=kind, document=_identity_of(backend)
        )
        return result

    @staticmethod
    async def _logged(context: MiddlewareContext, call_next, tool_name: str, start: float):
        try:
            result = await call_next(context)
            elapsed = (time.monotonic() - start) * 1000
            # A refusal comes back as a *returned* error result, not a raise
            # (see CapabilityRefusalMiddleware), so control flow alone would
            # log it as TOOL OK.
            if getattr(result, "is_error", False):
                log.warning("TOOL ERR %-40s %6.1fms  (refused)", tool_name, elapsed)
            else:
                log.info("TOOL OK  %-40s %6.1fms", tool_name, elapsed)
            return result
        except asyncio.CancelledError:
            # B01: a cancellation is a BaseException, so the handler below never
            # saw one and a cancelled call left no line in the audit log at all.
            elapsed = (time.monotonic() - start) * 1000
            log.warning("TOOL CANCELLED %-34s %6.1fms", tool_name, elapsed)
            raise
        except Exception as exc:
            elapsed = (time.monotonic() - start) * 1000
            log.warning("TOOL ERR %-40s %6.1fms  %s", tool_name, elapsed, exc)
            raise


#: Where an operation is recorded when no lifespan journal is reachable (a
#: direct call in a test or a script), so recording never fails a call.
_FALLBACK_OPERATIONS = OperationJournal()

#: ``call_outcomes`` outcomes meaning the worker ran on past the caller (a
#: cancellation or a deadline): a write may have landed.
_RAN_PAST_THE_CALLER = frozenset(
    {"cancelled_running", "completed_after_cancel", "failed_after_cancel", "abandoned_deadline"}
)

#: The record state for an error kind. Every kind not listed declined before
#: doing its work, so it is ``refused``.
_STATE_OF_KIND = {
    "failed": "failed",
    "timed_out": "failed",
    "quarantined": "quarantined",
    "unknown_commit": "unknown_commit",
    "cancelled": "cancelled",
}


def _lifespan_context_or_none(context) -> dict | None:
    try:
        lifespan = context.fastmcp_context.lifespan_context
    except Exception:  # no request context: a direct call
        return None
    return lifespan if isinstance(lifespan, dict) else None


def _operations_journal(lifespan) -> OperationJournal:
    journal = lifespan.get("operations") if isinstance(lifespan, dict) else None
    return journal if isinstance(journal, OperationJournal) else _FALLBACK_OPERATIONS


def _request_meta(context):
    """The request's ``_meta`` (a client's ``operation_id`` travels there), or None."""
    try:
        return context.fastmcp_context.request_context.meta
    except Exception:  # no request context: a direct call
        return None


def _effective_tool(tool: str, arguments: dict) -> str:
    """The tool a call really runs: the search proxy's ``call_tool`` names it."""
    if tool == "call_tool" and isinstance(arguments.get("name"), str):
        return arguments["name"]
    return tool


def _client_operation_id(tool: str, arguments: dict, meta) -> str | None:
    """A client's own id: ``_meta.operation_id``, or ``cad_batch``'s ``operation_id``."""
    from_meta = getattr(meta, "operation_id", None) if meta is not None else None
    from_args = None
    if tool == "cad_batch":
        from_args = arguments.get("operation_id")
    elif tool == "call_tool" and arguments.get("name") == "cad_batch":
        inner = arguments.get("arguments")
        from_args = inner.get("operation_id") if isinstance(inner, dict) else None
    if from_meta is not None and from_args is not None and from_meta != from_args:
        raise ValueError(
            f"operation_id: _meta.operation_id {from_meta!r} and cad_batch's operation_id "
            f"{from_args!r} disagree; send one id"
        )
    return from_meta if from_meta is not None else from_args


def _identity_of(backend) -> dict | None:
    reader = getattr(backend, "document_identity", None)
    if reader is None:
        return None
    try:
        return reader()
    except Exception:  # an identity is evidence, never a reason to fail a call
        return None


def _worker_outcome(backend, operation_id: str) -> str | None:
    """The latest ``call_outcomes`` outcome recorded under ``operation_id``, or None."""
    found = None
    for row in list(getattr(backend, "call_outcomes", None) or ()):
        if isinstance(row, dict) and row.get("operation_id") == operation_id:
            found = row.get("outcome")
    return found


def _read_only_tool(tool: str) -> bool:
    return tool in _batch_read_only_tools()


def _timeout_kind(backend, operation_id: str, tool: str) -> str | None:
    """``unknown_commit`` / ``timed_out`` when a worker outran its deadline, else None."""
    worker = _worker_outcome(backend, operation_id)
    if worker == "abandoned_deadline":
        return "timed_out" if _read_only_tool(tool) else "unknown_commit"
    if worker == "readonly_overrun":
        return "timed_out"
    return None


def _interrupted_state(backend, operation_id: str, tool: str) -> str:
    """A cancelled call: ``unknown_commit`` when a write's worker ran on, else ``cancelled``."""
    ran_on = _worker_outcome(backend, operation_id) in _RAN_PAST_THE_CALLER
    if ran_on and not _read_only_tool(tool):
        return "unknown_commit"
    return "cancelled"


def _exception_outcome(
    backend, operation_id: str, tool: str, exc: BaseException
) -> tuple[str, str]:
    timed = _timeout_kind(backend, operation_id, tool)
    if timed == "unknown_commit":
        return "unknown_commit", "timed_out"
    if timed == "timed_out":
        return "failed", "timed_out"
    kind = _classify_batch_error(exc)["kind"]
    return _STATE_OF_KIND.get(kind, "refused"), kind


def _result_outcome(tool: str, result) -> tuple[str, str | None]:
    structured = getattr(result, "structured_content", None)
    payload = structured if isinstance(structured, dict) else {}
    if getattr(result, "is_error", False):
        kind = str(payload.get("kind") or "refused")
        return ("quarantined" if kind == "quarantined" else "refused"), kind
    if tool == "cad_batch" and payload.get("dry_run") is False:
        if payload.get("ok") is True:
            return "committed", None
        first = next(
            (
                row["error"]["kind"]
                for row in payload.get("results", [])
                if row.get("status") == "error"
            ),
            "failed",
        )
        if (payload.get("atomicity") or {}).get("rolled_back") or not payload.get("succeeded"):
            return "failed", first
        return "partial", first
    if payload.get("ok") is False and not _read_only_tool(tool):
        return "refused", "refused"
    return "committed", None


def _replayed_result(stored, operation_id: str):
    """The recorded answer of ``operation_id``, marked ``replayed`` - nothing runs again."""
    if isinstance(stored, BaseException):
        raise ToolError(
            f"operation {operation_id!r} already ran and failed: {_refusal_message(stored)} "
            "(replayed, not run again; send a new operation_id to try again)"
        ) from stored
    structured = getattr(stored, "structured_content", None)
    if not isinstance(structured, dict):
        return stored
    payload = {**structured, "replayed": True, "operation_id": operation_id}
    return ToolResult(
        content=json.dumps(payload, ensure_ascii=False, default=str),
        structured_content=payload,
        is_error=bool(getattr(stored, "is_error", False)),
    )
```

4. Replace `CapabilityRefusalMiddleware.on_call_tool` (Task 9's version) with:

```python
    async def on_call_tool(self, context: MiddlewareContext, call_next):
        try:
            return await call_next(context)
        except Exception as exc:
            # B12: a refusal names the operation it ended.
            operation_id = CURRENT_OPERATION.get()
            changed = _document_changed_of(exc)
            if changed is not None:
                # B11: not a capability boundary and not a quarantine - the
                # operator switched AutoCAD's active document; nothing was written.
                payload = {
                    **changed,
                    "tool": context.message.name,
                    "backend": _backend_name_or_none(context),
                }
                if operation_id is not None:
                    payload["operation_id"] = operation_id
                return ToolResult(
                    content=payload["error"], structured_content=payload, is_error=True
                )
            capability = _batch_capability_of(exc)
            quarantine = None if capability else _batch_quarantine_of(exc)
            if capability is None and quarantine is None:
                raise  # not ours — leave it byte-for-byte as it was
            message = _refusal_message(exc)
            payload = {
                "ok": False,
                "error": message,
                "tool": context.message.name,
                "backend": _backend_name_or_none(context),
            }
            if operation_id is not None:
                payload["operation_id"] = operation_id
            if capability is not None:
                payload["kind"] = "unsupported"
                payload["capability"] = capability
            elif _batch_refusal_kind(exc) == "busy":
                # A detached read holds the document: not untrusted, busy.
                payload["kind"] = "busy"
                payload["detached_read"] = quarantine
            else:
                payload["kind"] = "quarantined"
                payload["quarantine"] = quarantine
            return ToolResult(
                content=message,  # prose-only clients still get a full sentence
                structured_content=payload,
                is_error=True,
            )
```

```python
BATCH_ERROR_KINDS = (
    "denied",  # on cad_batch's deny set; never executed
    "malformed_step",  # not a {tool, args, bind} object
    "unknown_tool",  # not registered, or hidden by TOOL_PROFILE / TOOL_PACKS / ENABLE_3D
    "invalid_args",  # rejected by the tool's own JSON Schema
    "unresolved_ref",  # a $reference that no earlier step successfully bound
    "unsupported",  # this backend cannot do it (carries `capability`)
    "refused",  # the tool declined on purpose (path/command validation, ...)
    "failed",  # attempted and broke
    "quarantined",  # the document is quarantined after an abandoned call (R32)
    "busy",  # a detached read holds the document (W1 review follow-up)
    "stale_revision",  # the document moved since its preview (Task 12)
    "active_document_changed",  # live engine: the operator switched documents (B11)
    "scan_incomplete",  # a QA or verification read could not read everything (B02)
    "unknown_commit",  # a write that outran its deadline: it may have landed (B12)
    "timed_out",  # a read that outran its deadline
    "cancelled",  # the request was cancelled
    "operation_conflict",  # an operation id reused for another request (B12)
)
```

8. In the step loop, replace everything from

```python

                # run_middleware=False: the cad_batch call itself is already through
```

through

```python

```

with:

```python
            # B12: a step is an operation of its own. Steps skip the middleware,
            # so the id is minted here and set for the step's backend calls,
            # whose call_outcomes rows then carry it.
            step, _ = journal.begin(name, args, document=_identity_of(backend))
            step_token = CURRENT_OPERATION.set(step.operation_id)
            try:
                # run_middleware=False: the cad_batch call itself is already through
                # the audit/timing chain, and skipping it keeps the error one wrap
                # deep instead of three, which is what makes the taxonomy above
                # readable. Each step is logged here instead, tagged as a batch step.
                outcome = await ctx.fastmcp.call_tool(name, args, run_middleware=False)
            except asyncio.CancelledError:
                journal.finish(
                    step,
                    _interrupted_state(backend, step.operation_id, name),
                    error_kind="cancelled",
                    document=_identity_of(backend),
                )
                raise
            except Exception as exc:
                failed += 1
                executed += 1
                row["status"] = "error"
                row["error"] = _classify_batch_error(exc)
                timed = _timeout_kind(backend, step.operation_id, name)
                if timed is not None:
                    row["error"]["kind"] = timed
                row["error"]["operation_id"] = step.operation_id
                kind = row["error"]["kind"]
                journal.finish(
                    step,
                    _STATE_OF_KIND.get(kind, "refused"),
                    error_kind=kind,
                    document=_identity_of(backend),
                )
                log.warning("BATCH %d/%d %-38s %s", position + 1, total, name, kind)
                results.append(row)
                if mode == "rollback":
                    break
                continue
            finally:
                CURRENT_OPERATION.reset(step_token)
            executed += 1
            structured = getattr(outcome, "structured_content", None)
            # A tool may decline *by value* instead of raising: an `is_error` result
            # from the refusal middleware, or an `{"ok": False, ...}` payload. Both
            # mean the step did not do its work, and counting either as succeeded is
            # the same class of lie as the DWG bytes and the unverified rollback —
            # the batch would report succeeded:1 over work that never happened.
            #
            # `ok: False` only carries that meaning for a tool that was supposed to
            # ACT. On a read-only checker it means "I looked and found something",
            # and treating that as a refusal is how a `validation_check` step came
            # to trigger a rollback that destroyed the batch's own geometry.
            refused = bool(getattr(outcome, "is_error", False)) or (
                name not in read_only
                and isinstance(structured, dict)
                and structured.get("ok") is False
            )
            if refused:
                failed += 1
                declined_payload = structured if isinstance(structured, dict) else {}
                capability = declined_payload.get("capability")
                row["status"] = "error"
                row["error"] = {
                    "kind": "unsupported" if capability else "refused",
                    **({"capability": capability} if capability else {}),
                    "message": _compact_batch_message(
                        str(declined_payload.get("error") or "the tool declined")
                    ),
                    "operation_id": step.operation_id,
                }
                journal.finish(
                    step,
                    "refused",
                    error_kind=row["error"]["kind"],
                    document=_identity_of(backend),
                )
                log.warning("BATCH %d/%d %-38s %s", position + 1, total, name, row["error"]["kind"])
                results.append(row)
                # `failed` is what on_error='stop' reads at the top of the loop, so
                # incrementing it above is the whole stop mechanism.
                if mode == "rollback":
                    break
                continue
            succeeded += 1
            journal.finish(step, "committed", document=_identity_of(backend))
            if plan["_bind"]:
                bindings[plan["_bind"]] = structured
                row["bind"] = plan["_bind"]
            row["status"] = "ok"
            row["result"] = _compact_batch_result(structured, verbose)
            log.info("BATCH %d/%d %-38s ok", position + 1, total, name)
            results.append(row)
```

```bash
git -c user.name="Umut CAN" -c user.email="umutcan.edizaslan@gmail.com" commit -F - <<'EOF'
feat(operations): operation records, client ids and the 1.7 result taxonomy (B12)

backends/operations.py keeps one record per call in a bounded in-memory
journal: operation_id, tool, request_hash (sha256 of the canonical JSON of
tool + arguments), state, document id and the revision before and after,
started / ended, error kind. States: committed, partial, failed, refused,
quarantined, cancelled, unknown_commit (a mutating call cancelled or timed out
while its worker ran).

AuditMiddleware mints the id into CURRENT_OPERATION (the search proxy's
call_tool re-enters and reuses it), cad_batch mints one per step (its steps
skip the middleware), and ComBackend._run carries it into the STA job
explicitly; every call_outcomes row now names its operation, and a COM
deadline overrun leaves an abandoned_deadline row.

Client ids - cad_batch(operation_id=...) and _meta.operation_id on any tool:
the same id and request hash replays the recorded result (replayed: true),
an unknown_commit refuses requires_reconcile, a different request refuses
operation_conflict. The journal is in memory (B13 makes it durable in 1.8.0).

BATCH_ERROR_KINDS gains quarantined and busy (both emitted, never declared),
stale_revision, active_document_changed, scan_incomplete, unknown_commit,
timed_out, cancelled, operation_conflict; refusals and step errors carry
operation_id; system_status lists the recent operations.
No backlog row of docs/analysis/backlog-1.7.0-triage.json is owned by Task 10.

Known red: tests/test_release_consistency.py::test_document_numbers_match_the_tree
(README's collected-test figure; Task 17 retakes it).
```

- Beyond the skeleton (Tasks 13-17 may use them; recorded): `SelectorKind`, `FeatureType`, `BindingStatus` literals and the tuples `STATUSES`, `PROVENANCES`, `QUANTITIES`, `SELECTOR_KINDS`, `FEATURE_TYPES`, `BINDING_STATUSES`; `SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`; `FrozenMap`, `freeze(mapping) -> pairs`, `thaw(pairs) -> dict`; `to_dict()` on every type; `RequirementSet.to_dict()` (`{"version", "units", "items" (sorted by id), "legacy"}`) and `RequirementSet.by_id()`; construction refuses an unknown vocabulary word with `ValueError`; `RequirementError(SpecError)`, `requirement_conflicts(rs) -> list[dict]`, `encode_requirements(rs) -> list[str]`, `decode_requirements(chunks) -> RequirementSet`, `DICT_NAME = "ACADMCP_REQUIREMENTS"`, `RECORD_NAME = "CURRENT"`, `CHUNK_SIZE = 255`, `LENGTH_UNITS`, `ANGLE_UNITS`, `FEATURE_ARITY`; `PreflightResult.requirement_set: dict | None`, `PreflightResult.legacy: list[dict]`; `PlanSpec.requirement_digest: str | None`, `PlanSpec.requirements_persisted: bool`.
- Selector vocabulary (aligned with Task 14's binder, recorded): `handle` `{handle, block_path?}`; `dimension` `{handle}`; `feature_tag` `{part, feature, view?, type?, fallback?}` (`fallback` is a predicate used on a drawing without feature tags); `arch_element` `{element, type?}`; `predicate` `{type, layer?, space?, region?, radius_min?, radius_max?, length_min?, length_max?}` (`PREDICATE_KEYS`). The draft's `size_min` / `size_max` window and `arch_element.role` are replaced by these keys: Task 14 reads `radius_*` / `length_*`, `space`, `block_path`, `type` and `fallback`, and a key the parser refused could never reach it. The parser keeps `type` required on a predicate and `part` on a feature tag; the binder also accepts them absent, which only hand-built selectors use.
- Raw input shape (recorded): a set is `{"units": ..., "items": [...], "dimensions": {...}}` — the typed list is `items` (the tool parameter is already called `requirements`); `to_dict()` writes the legacy object back as `legacy`, which the parser also reads.

Create `tests/test_verification_types.py`:

```python
"""B15 residue - the shared verification types, defined once for Tasks 11-17.

The digest's oracle is the canonical JSON written out by hand below; the
vocabularies are pinned word for word, because a later task that misspells a
status must fail at construction instead of passing silently.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import dataclasses
import hashlib
import json

import pytest

from engineering.verification.types import (
    BINDING_STATUSES,
    FEATURE_TYPES,
    PROVENANCES,
    QUANTITIES,
    SELECTOR_KINDS,
    STATUSES,
    AcceptanceItem,
    Binding,
    FeatureRef,
    FeatureSelector,
    Measurement,
    ObservedFeature,
    ObservedGeometry,
    Requirement,
    RequirementSet,
    freeze,
    thaw,
)


def _req(**changes) -> Requirement:
    fields = {
        "id": "D1",
        "quantity": "diameter",
        "target": freeze({"nominal": 40.0, "unit": "mm"}),
        "features": (
            FeatureSelector("feature_tag", freeze({"part": "flange", "feature": "bore"})),
        ),
        "provenance": "explicit",
        "source": freeze({"page": 1, "label": "Ø40"}),
    }
    fields.update(changes)
    return Requirement(**fields)


def test_the_vocabularies_are_closed_and_spelled_once():
    assert STATUSES == ("pass", "fail", "unknown", "unsupported", "ambiguous")
    assert PROVENANCES == (
        "explicit",
        "derived",
        "assumed",
        "conflicting",
        "unknown",
        "raster_estimate",
    )
    assert QUANTITIES == (
        "distance",
        "radius",
        "diameter",
        "angle",
        "count",
        "absence",
        "concentric",
        "coincident",
        "parallel",
        "perpendicular",
        "tangent",
        "horizontal",
        "vertical",
    )
    assert SELECTOR_KINDS == ("handle", "feature_tag", "arch_element", "predicate", "dimension")
    assert FEATURE_TYPES == ("line", "circle", "arc", "ellipse", "polyline", "point")
    assert BINDING_STATUSES == ("bound", "ambiguous", "unknown")


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "build, message",
    [
        (
            lambda: Measurement("D1", 40.0, "mm", "radius", 0.0, "passed"),
            "Measurement.status must be one of",
        ),
        (lambda: Binding("D1", "picked", (), (), "x"), "Binding.status must be one of"),
        (lambda: FeatureSelector("layer", ()), "FeatureSelector.kind must be one of"),
        (
            lambda: ObservedFeature("1A", "spline", "Model", "0", (), ()),
            "ObservedFeature.type must be one of",
        ),
        (lambda: _req(quantity="volume"), "Requirement.quantity must be one of"),
        (lambda: _req(provenance="guessed"), "Requirement.provenance must be one of"),
    ],
)
def test_an_unknown_word_is_refused_at_construction(build, message):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match=message):
        build()


def test_requirements_are_hashable_and_frozen():
    requirement = _req()
    assert {requirement, _req()} == {requirement}
    with pytest.raises(dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError):
        requirement.id = "D2"


def test_freeze_and_thaw_round_trip_nested_values():
    frozen = freeze({"b": [1, 2, {"c": 3}], "a": {"z": (4.0, 5.0)}})
    assert [key for key, _ in frozen] == ["a", "b"], "sorted by key"
    assert hash(frozen) == hash(freeze({"a": {"z": [4.0, 5.0]}, "b": [1, 2, {"c": 3}]}))
    assert thaw(frozen) == {"a": {"z": [4.0, 5.0]}, "b": [1, 2, {"c": 3}]}


def test_digest_is_the_sha256_of_the_canonical_json():
    count = Requirement(
        id="H1",
        quantity="count",
        target=freeze({"exact": 6}),
        features=(
            FeatureSelector(
                "predicate", freeze({"type": "circle", "radius_min": 9.0, "radius_max": 11.0})
            ),
        ),
        provenance="explicit",
        source=freeze({"label": "6x Ø10"}),
    )
    rs = RequirementSet(requirements=(count, _req()), units="mm")
    canonical = {
        "version": 1,
        "units": "mm",
        "legacy": None,
        "items": [
            {
                "id": "D1",
                "quantity": "diameter",
                "target": {"nominal": 40.0, "unit": "mm"},
                "features": [{"kind": "feature_tag", "part": "flange", "feature": "bore"}],
                "provenance": "explicit",
                "source": {"page": 1, "label": "Ø40"},
                "datum": None,
                "manufacturing_tolerance": None,
                "verification_epsilon": None,
            },
            {
                "id": "H1",
                "quantity": "count",
                "target": {"exact": 6},
                "features": [
                    {"kind": "predicate", "type": "circle", "radius_min": 9.0, "radius_max": 11.0}
                ],
                "provenance": "explicit",
                "source": {"label": "6x Ø10"},
                "datum": None,
                "manufacturing_tolerance": None,
                "verification_epsilon": None,
            },
        ],
    }
    text = json.dumps(canonical, sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)
    assert rs.to_dict() == canonical
    assert rs.digest() == "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()
    reordered = RequirementSet(requirements=tuple(reversed(rs.requirements)), units="mm")
    assert reordered.digest() == rs.digest(), "input order is no part of a set"
    assert rs.by_id()["H1"] is count


def test_the_evidence_types_serialise_to_plain_json():
    ref = FeatureRef(
        document_id="a" * 32,
        revision=3,
        space="Model",
        block_path=("FLANGE",),
        handles=("2A",),
        feature="bore",
    )
    binding = Binding("D1", "bound", (ref,), (ref,), "feature tag flange/bore")
    measurement = Measurement(
        "D1", 40.02, "mm", "circle.radius*2", 0.02, "pass", detail=freeze({"epsilon": 1e-6})
    )
    item = AcceptanceItem(_req(), binding, measurement, delta=0.02, tolerance_basis="fit H7")
    feature = ObservedFeature(
        "2A",
        "circle",
        "Model",
        "GEOMETRY",
        ("FLANGE",),
        freeze({"center": [0.0, 0.0], "radius": 20.01}),
        tags=(("ACADMCP_MECH", '{"kind":"feature"}'),),
    )
    observed = ObservedGeometry((feature,), "mm", "mm", "a" * 32, 3, "memory:untitled")
    assert ref.to_dict() == {
        "document_id": "a" * 32,
        "revision": 3,
        "space": "Model",
        "block_path": ["FLANGE"],
        "handles": ["2A"],
        "feature": "bore",
    }
    assert binding.to_dict() == {
        "requirement_id": "D1",
        "status": "bound",
        "refs": [ref.to_dict()],
        "candidates": [ref.to_dict()],
        "reason": "feature tag flange/bore",
    }
    assert measurement.to_dict() == {
        "requirement_id": "D1",
        "value": 40.02,
        "unit": "mm",
        "method": "circle.radius*2",
        "residual": 0.02,
        "status": "pass",
        "detail": {"epsilon": 1e-6},
    }
    assert item.to_dict() == {
        "requirement": _req().to_dict(),
        "binding": binding.to_dict(),
        "measurement": measurement.to_dict(),
        "delta": 0.02,
        "tolerance_basis": "fit H7",
    }
    assert feature.to_dict() == {
        "handle": "2A",
        "type": "circle",
        "space": "Model",
        "layer": "GEOMETRY",
        "block_path": ["FLANGE"],
        "geometry": {"center": [0.0, 0.0], "radius": 20.01},
        "tags": [["ACADMCP_MECH", '{"kind":"feature"}']],
        "approximation": None,
    }
    assert observed.to_dict() == {
        "features": [feature.to_dict()],
        "declared_unit": "mm",
        "inferred_unit": "mm",
        "document_id": "a" * 32,
        "revision": 3,
        "source": "memory:untitled",
    }
    assert json.loads(json.dumps(item.to_dict())) == item.to_dict()
    assert json.loads(json.dumps(observed.to_dict())) == observed.to_dict()
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the shared types**

Create `engineering/verification/types.py`:

```python
"""The shared verification types (v1.7.0, B15 residue), defined once for Tasks 11-17.

Frozen, hashable dataclasses. A mapping is stored as sorted ``(key, value)``
pairs (``freeze``) so a requirement can be a dict key or a set member, and
``to_dict`` gives the JSON form back (``thaw``). The vocabularies are closed:
an unknown status, quantity, provenance, selector kind, feature type or
binding status is a ``ValueError`` at construction, so a typo can never
become a silent pass.

A verdict (``Status``) is one of:

    pass, fail      measured on CAD-read geometry against the target
    unknown         no binding, or nothing it could be measured on
    unsupported     a geometry the evaluator cannot measure (a spline, a tilted plane)
    ambiguous       two equal candidates; never a pick

A missing requirement is never ``pass``. ``manufacturing_tolerance`` (with its
own source) and ``verification_epsilon`` (numeric noise) are separate fields
and are never merged.

Pure: no backend, no I/O.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import hashlib
import json
from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Literal, get_args

__all__ = [
    "BINDING_STATUSES",
    "FEATURE_TYPES",
    "PROVENANCES",
    "QUANTITIES",
    "SCHEMA_VERSION",
    "SELECTOR_KINDS",
    "STATUSES",
    "AcceptanceItem",
    "Binding",
    "BindingStatus",
    "FeatureRef",
    "FeatureSelector",
    "FeatureType",
    "FrozenMap",
    "Measurement",
    "ObservedFeature",
    "ObservedGeometry",
    "Provenance",
    "Quantity",
    "Requirement",
    "RequirementSet",
    "SelectorKind",
    "Status",
    "freeze",
    "thaw",
]

Status = Literal["pass", "fail", "unknown", "unsupported", "ambiguous"]
Provenance = Literal["explicit", "derived", "assumed", "conflicting", "unknown", "raster_estimate"]
Quantity = Literal[
    "distance",
    "radius",
    "diameter",
    "angle",
    "count",
    "absence",
    "concentric",
    "coincident",
    "parallel",
    "perpendicular",
    "tangent",
    "horizontal",
    "vertical",
]
SelectorKind = Literal["handle", "feature_tag", "arch_element", "predicate", "dimension"]
FeatureType = Literal["line", "circle", "arc", "ellipse", "polyline", "point"]
BindingStatus = Literal["bound", "ambiguous", "unknown"]

STATUSES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(Status)
PROVENANCES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(Provenance)
QUANTITIES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(Quantity)
SELECTOR_KINDS: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(SelectorKind)
FEATURE_TYPES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(FeatureType)
BINDING_STATUSES: tuple[str, ...] = get_args(BindingStatus)

#: The version of ``RequirementSet.to_dict()`` (and so of its digest).
SCHEMA_VERSION = 1

Pairs = tuple[tuple[str, object], ...]


class FrozenMap(tuple):
    """A frozen mapping: sorted ``(key, value)`` pairs that ``thaw`` turns back into a dict.

    A tuple, so it hashes and compares like one; its own type is what lets
    ``thaw`` tell a nested mapping from a nested list.
    """

    __slots__ = ()


def _freeze_value(value):
    if isinstance(value, FrozenMap):
        return value
    if isinstance(value, Mapping):
        return FrozenMap(_pairs(value))
    if isinstance(value, (list, tuple)):
        return tuple(_freeze_value(item) for item in value)
    return value


def _pairs(mapping: Mapping) -> Pairs:
    return tuple((str(key), _freeze_value(mapping[key])) for key in sorted(mapping, key=str))


def freeze(mapping: Mapping | None) -> Pairs:
    """``mapping`` as sorted pairs; nested mappings become ``FrozenMap``, lists tuples."""
    if mapping is None:
        return FrozenMap(())
    if not isinstance(mapping, Mapping):
        raise TypeError(f"freeze takes a mapping, got {type(mapping).__name__}")
    return FrozenMap(_pairs(mapping))


def _thaw_value(value):
    if isinstance(value, FrozenMap):
        return {key: _thaw_value(item) for key, item in value}
    if isinstance(value, tuple):
        return [_thaw_value(item) for item in value]
    return value


def thaw(pairs: Pairs | None) -> dict:
    """The dict ``freeze`` made ``pairs`` from (tuples come back as lists)."""
    return {str(key): _thaw_value(value) for key, value in (pairs or ())}


def _check(value, choices: tuple[str, ...], what: str) -> None:
    if value not in choices:
        raise ValueError(f"{what} must be one of {list(choices)}, got {value!r}")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FeatureSelector:
    kind: SelectorKind
    value: Pairs

    def __post_init__(self):
        _check(self.kind, SELECTOR_KINDS, "FeatureSelector.kind")

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {"kind": self.kind, **thaw(self.value)}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Requirement:
    id: str
    quantity: Quantity
    target: Pairs
    features: tuple[FeatureSelector, ...]
    provenance: Provenance
    source: Pairs
    datum: str | None = None
    manufacturing_tolerance: Pairs | None = None
    verification_epsilon: float | None = None

    def __post_init__(self):
        _check(self.quantity, QUANTITIES, "Requirement.quantity")
        _check(self.provenance, PROVENANCES, "Requirement.provenance")

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "id": self.id,
            "quantity": self.quantity,
            "target": thaw(self.target),
            "features": [selector.to_dict() for selector in self.features],
            "provenance": self.provenance,
            "source": thaw(self.source),
            "datum": self.datum,
            "manufacturing_tolerance": None
            if self.manufacturing_tolerance is None
            else thaw(self.manufacturing_tolerance),
            "verification_epsilon": self.verification_epsilon,
        }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RequirementSet:
    requirements: tuple[Requirement, ...]
    units: str
    legacy: Pairs | None = None

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        """The canonical form: items sorted by id, so the digest ignores input order."""
        return {
            "version": SCHEMA_VERSION,
            "units": self.units,
            "items": [r.to_dict() for r in sorted(self.requirements, key=lambda r: r.id)],
            "legacy": None if self.legacy is None else thaw(self.legacy),
        }

    def digest(self) -> str:
        """``sha256:`` + the hex digest of the canonical JSON of ``to_dict()``."""
        text = json.dumps(self.to_dict(), sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":"), ensure_ascii=False)
        return "sha256:" + hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()

    def by_id(self) -> dict[str, Requirement]:
        return {requirement.id: requirement for requirement in self.requirements}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ObservedFeature:
    handle: str
    type: FeatureType
    space: str
    layer: str
    block_path: tuple[str, ...]
    geometry: Pairs
    tags: tuple[tuple[str, str], ...] = ()
    approximation: str | None = None

    def __post_init__(self):
        _check(self.type, FEATURE_TYPES, "ObservedFeature.type")

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "handle": self.handle,
            "type": self.type,
            "space": self.space,
            "layer": self.layer,
            "block_path": list(self.block_path),
            "geometry": thaw(self.geometry),
            "tags": [[app, payload] for app, payload in self.tags],
            "approximation": self.approximation,
        }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ObservedGeometry:
    features: tuple[ObservedFeature, ...]
    declared_unit: str | None
    inferred_unit: str | None
    document_id: str | None
    revision: int | None
    source: str

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "features": [feature.to_dict() for feature in self.features],
            "declared_unit": self.declared_unit,
            "inferred_unit": self.inferred_unit,
            "document_id": self.document_id,
            "revision": self.revision,
            "source": self.source,
        }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FeatureRef:
    document_id: str | None
    revision: int | None
    space: str
    block_path: tuple[str, ...]
    handles: tuple[str, ...]
    feature: str | None

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "document_id": self.document_id,
            "revision": self.revision,
            "space": self.space,
            "block_path": list(self.block_path),
            "handles": list(self.handles),
            "feature": self.feature,
        }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Binding:
    requirement_id: str
    status: BindingStatus
    refs: tuple[FeatureRef, ...]
    candidates: tuple[FeatureRef, ...]
    reason: str

    def __post_init__(self):
        _check(self.status, BINDING_STATUSES, "Binding.status")

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "requirement_id": self.requirement_id,
            "status": self.status,
            "refs": [ref.to_dict() for ref in self.refs],
            "candidates": [ref.to_dict() for ref in self.candidates],
            "reason": self.reason,
        }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Measurement:
    requirement_id: str
    value: float | None
    unit: str
    method: str
    residual: float | None
    status: Status
    detail: Pairs = ()

    def __post_init__(self):
        _check(self.status, STATUSES, "Measurement.status")

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "requirement_id": self.requirement_id,
            "value": self.value,
            "unit": self.unit,
            "method": self.method,
            "residual": self.residual,
            "status": self.status,
            "detail": thaw(self.detail),
        }


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class AcceptanceItem:
    requirement: Requirement
    binding: Binding
    measurement: Measurement
    delta: float | None
    tolerance_basis: str

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "requirement": self.requirement.to_dict(),
            "binding": self.binding.to_dict(),
            "measurement": self.measurement.to_dict(),
            "delta": self.delta,
            "tolerance_basis": self.tolerance_basis,
        }
```

Create `engineering/verification/__init__.py`:

```python
"""Verification engine (v1.7.0): requirements bound to CAD features, measured, reported.

Task 11 lands the shared types (``types``) and the requirement parser and
store (``requirements``); ``observe``, ``binding``, ``measure``, ``report``
and ``artifact`` join in Tasks 13-17.
"""

from engineering.verification.types import (
    BINDING_STATUSES,
    FEATURE_TYPES,
    PROVENANCES,
    QUANTITIES,
    SCHEMA_VERSION,
    SELECTOR_KINDS,
    STATUSES,
    AcceptanceItem,
    Binding,
    FeatureRef,
    FeatureSelector,
    Measurement,
    ObservedFeature,
    ObservedGeometry,
    Requirement,
    RequirementSet,
    freeze,
    thaw,
)

__all__ = [
    "BINDING_STATUSES",
    "FEATURE_TYPES",
    "PROVENANCES",
```

```python
"""B15 residue - typed requirements: parsed once, refused by their path, conflicts kept.

Every expected value is written out by hand; nothing the parser returns is
used to build its own oracle.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

import config
from engineering.validation import SpecError
from engineering.verification.requirements import (
    CHUNK_SIZE,
    decode_requirements,
    encode_requirements,
    parse_requirements,
    requirement_conflicts,
)
from engineering.verification.types import FeatureSelector, Requirement, freeze, thaw


def _item(**changes) -> dict:
    item = {
        "id": "D1",
        "quantity": "diameter",
        "target": {"nominal": 40},
        "features": [{"kind": "feature_tag", "part": "flange", "feature": "bore"}],
        "provenance": "explicit",
    }
    item.update(changes)
    return item


def test_a_typed_set_is_canonicalised():
    rs = parse_requirements(
        {
            "units": "Millimetre",
            "items": [
                _item(
                    id="N1",
                    quantity="count",
                    target={"exact": 6},
                    features=[
                        {
                            "kind": "predicate",
                            "type": "circle",
                            "radius_min": 9,
                            "radius_max": 11,
                            "region": [100, 50, -100, -50],
                        }
                    ],
                ),
                _item(
                    target={"nominal": 40, "unit": "inch"},
                    features=[{"kind": "handle", "handle": "2a"}],
                    source={"page": 2, "label": "Ø40"},
                    manufacturing_tolerance={"fit": "H7", "source": "drawing note 3"},
                    verification_epsilon=0.001,
                ),
            ],
        }
    )
    assert rs.units == "mm"
    assert [r.id for r in rs.requirements] == ["D1", "N1"], "sorted by id"
    d1, n1 = rs.requirements
    assert d1 == Requirement(
        id="D1",
        quantity="diameter",
        target=freeze({"nominal": 40.0, "unit": "in"}),
        features=(FeatureSelector("handle", freeze({"handle": "2A"})),),
        provenance="explicit",
        source=freeze({"label": "Ø40", "page": 2}),
        manufacturing_tolerance=freeze({"fit": "H7", "source": "drawing note 3"}),
        verification_epsilon=0.001,
    )
    assert thaw(n1.target) == {"exact": 6}
    assert thaw(n1.features[0].value) == {
        "type": "circle",
        "radius_min": 9.0,
        "radius_max": 11.0,
        "region": [-100.0, -50.0, 100.0, 50.0],
    }
    assert parse_requirements(rs.to_dict()) == rs, "the canonical form parses back to itself"


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    "items, message",
    [
        (
            [_item(target={"nominal": -1})],
            r"items\[0\]\.target\.nominal: must be greater than zero, got -1\.0",
        ),
        (
            [
                _item(

                    features=[

                        {"kind": "handle", "handle": "1B"},
                    ],
                )
            ],
            r"items\[0\]\.target\.nominal: nan is not a finite number",
        ),
        ([_item(quantity="volume")], r"items\[0\]\.quantity: must be one of"),
        (
            [{key: value for key, value in _item().items() if key != "provenance"}],
            r"items\[0\]\.provenance: required",
        ),
        (
            [_item(quantity="count", target={"exact": 6, "min": 2})],
            r"items\[0\]\.target: 'exact' excludes 'min' and 'max'",
        ),
        (
            [_item(quantity="concentric", target=None)],
            r"items\[0\]\.features: a concentric takes 2 selector\(s\), got 1",
        ),
        (
            [_item(manufacturing_tolerance={"upper": 0.02, "lower": 0.0})],
            r"items\[0\]\.manufacturing_tolerance\.source: a manufacturing tolerance names its own",
        ),
        ([_item(), _item()], r"items\[1\]\.id: 'D1' is already items\[0\]\.id"),
        (
            [
                _item(
                    features=[
                        {"kind": "feature_tag", "part": "flange", "feature": "bore", "colour": 1}
                    ]
                )

            r"items\[0\]\.features\[0\]: unknown key\(s\) \['colour'\]",

        (

        ),
        (
            [_item(quantity="horizontal", target={"nominal": 0})],
            r"items\[0\]\.target: a horizontal requirement takes no target",
        ),
    ],
)
def test_a_malformed_item_is_refused_by_its_path(items, message):
    with pytest.raises(SpecError, match=message):
        parse_requirements({"units": "mm", "items": items})


def test_the_selector_vocabulary_is_the_binders():
    """Task 14's binder reads exactly these keys; the parser passes them through."""
    rs = parse_requirements(
        {
            "units": "mm",
            "items": [
                _item(features=[{"kind": "handle", "handle": "2a", "block_path": ["1f", "20"]}]),
                _item(
                    id="D2",
                    features=[
                        {
                            "kind": "feature_tag",
                            "part": "flange",
                            "feature": "bore",
                            "view": "front",
                            "type": "circle",
                            "fallback": {"type": "circle", "radius_min": 19.9, "radius_max": 20.1},
                        }
                    ],
                ),
                _item(
                    id="D3", features=[{"kind": "arch_element", "element": "door:D1", "type": "arc"}]
                ),
                _item(
                    id="L1",
                    quantity="distance",
                    target={"nominal": 100},
                    features=[
                        {

                            "type": "line",
                            "layer": "GEOMETRY",
                            "space": "Model",
                            "length_min": 99,
                            "length_max": 101,
                        }
                    ],
                ),
            ],
        }
    )
    d1, d2, d3, l1 = rs.requirements
    assert thaw(d1.features[0].value) == {"handle": "2A", "block_path": ["1F", "20"]}
    assert thaw(d2.features[0].value)["fallback"] == {
        "type": "circle",
        "radius_min": 19.9,
        "radius_max": 20.1,
    }
    assert thaw(d3.features[0].value) == {"element": "door:D1", "type": "arc"}
    assert thaw(l1.features[0].value) == {
        "type": "line",
        "layer": "GEOMETRY",
        "space": "Model",
        "length_min": 99.0,
        "length_max": 101.0,
    }
    with pytest.raises(
        SpecError,
        match=r"items\[0\]\.features\[0\]: radius_min 2\.0 is greater than radius_max 1\.0",
    ):
        parse_requirements(
            {
                "units": "mm",
                "items": [
                    _item(
                        features=[
                            {"kind": "predicate", "type": "circle", "radius_min": 2, "radius_max": 1}
                        ]
                    )
                ],
            }
        )


def test_units_are_required():
    with pytest.raises(SpecError, match=r"units: required"):
        parse_requirements({"items": [_item()]})


def test_nothing_to_read_is_refused():
    with pytest.raises(SpecError, match="nothing to read"):
        parse_requirements({"units": "mm"})


def test_the_item_count_is_budgeted_by_max_spec_items(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(config.settings, "max_spec_items", 2)
    items = [_item(id=f"D{index}") for index in range(3)]
    with pytest.raises(SpecError, match=r"items: 3 is over the limit of 2; set MAX_SPEC_ITEMS"):
        parse_requirements({"units": "mm", "items": items})


def test_a_legacy_dimensions_object_is_kept_as_given():
    rs = parse_requirements(
        {"units": "mm", "dimensions": {"width": 80, "holes": {"count": 6, "pcd": 120}}}
    )
    assert rs.requirements == ()
    assert thaw(rs.legacy) == {"width": 80, "holes": {"count": 6, "pcd": 120}}


def test_conflicts_are_reported_and_both_requirements_kept():
    rs = parse_requirements(
        {
            "units": "mm",
            "items": [_item(), _item(id="D2", target={"nominal": 42}, provenance="conflicting")],
        }
    )
    assert [r.id for r in rs.requirements] == ["D1", "D2"]
    assert [thaw(r.target)["nominal"] for r in rs.requirements] == [40.0, 42.0]
    assert [(c["code"], c["values"]) for c in requirement_conflicts(rs)] == [
        ("CONFLICTING_PROVENANCE", ["D2"]),
        ("CONFLICTING_REQUIREMENT", ["D1", "D2"]),
    ]


def test_the_stored_form_is_ascii_chunks_and_round_trips():
    items = [_item(id=f"D{index}", source={"label": "Ø40 " * 10}) for index in range(10)]
    rs = parse_requirements({"units": "mm", "items": items})
    chunks = encode_requirements(rs)
    assert len(chunks) > 1
    assert all(len(chunk) <= CHUNK_SIZE and chunk.isascii() for chunk in chunks)
    assert decode_requirements(chunks) == rs


def test_an_edited_record_is_refused_by_its_digest():
    rs = parse_requirements({"units": "mm", "items": [_item()]})
    text = "".join(encode_requirements(rs))
    assert '"nominal":40.0' in text
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="does not match"):
        decode_requirements([text.replace('"nominal":40.0', '"nominal":42.0')])
```
